Section
B.4
:
Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
(Lemma
B.4
)

B.1
Consistency of Policy Parameters (Theorem
B.1
)

Theorem B.1

(Consistency of Policy Parameters)
.

We assume Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Function) hold.
Condition
1
holds (i.e.,
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{t}^{*}
for each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
),
under the following additional assumptions:

(CP1)

Well-Separated Solutions:

For each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists some
\eta>0
such that

\inf_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{*}\|_{1}>\epsilon}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big]\right\|_{1}>\eta>0.

(CP2)

Asymptotically Tight:

For each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists some
k<\infty
such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\epsilon.

(CP3)

Finite Bracketing Number:

Let
\alpha>0
be a constant. For each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
and any compact subset
K_{t}\subset\real^{d_{t}}
,

(i)

For any
\epsilon>0
and any vector
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
, the bracketing number

N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t})\big\}_{\beta_{t}\in K_{t}},~L_{1+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)<\infty.

(ii)

There exists a function
F_{\phi_{t}}
such that for all
\beta_{t}\in K_{t}
,
\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big\|_{1}\leq F_{\phi_{t}}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
a.s. and

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big|F_{\phi_{t}}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\big|^{1+\alpha}\right]<\infty

for all
t^{\prime}\in[2\colon t]
; the functions
\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}
are from Condition
3
.

Proof of Theorem
B.1
(Consistency of Policy Parameters).
We use an induction-based argument. For the base case, we show that
\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1}^{*}
. For the induction step, we show that
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{t}^{*}
, given that
\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}
.

Base Case.

Note that
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(2)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(3)},\dots,\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(n)}
are i.i.d. (data from the first time-step; no adaptive sampling yet). This type of consistency proof is standard for Z-estimators; we include it for completeness since the induction step uses similar techniques.

We first define the following useful functions:

\Phi_{1}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\triangleq\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}\big)\big]~~~~~~\textnormal{and}~~~~~~\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}\big).

Let
\epsilon>0
. By assumption
(CP1)
, there exists some
\eta>0
such that if
\beta_{1}\in\real^{d_{1}}
satisfies
\big\|\beta_{1}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon
, then
\big\|\Phi_{1}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}\big)\big]\big\|_{1}>\eta>0
. Thus,
\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\|_{1}>\epsilon}\leq\mathbb{I}_{\|\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\|_{1}>\eta}
, so

\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta\right).

By the definition of
\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}
from display (
3.9
),
\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\\
=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})=o_{P}(1)
. Thus,

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right).

By assumption
(CP2)
, for any
\delta>0
, there exists some
k<\infty
such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
. We use this
k
below:

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\left\{\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}+\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}\right\}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)+o(1)

\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\beta_{1}\in\real^{d_{1}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{1}\|_{1}\leq k}\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\bigg)}_{=o(1)}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)}_{\leq\delta}+o(1).

(B.1)

For the second term above, by assumption
(CP2)
,
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
and
\delta
can be made arbitrarily small.

For the first term above, we can apply the Uniform Weak Law of Large Numbers result for i.i.d. data
[
Van Der Vaart and Wellner 1996
, Theorem 2.4.1]

to get that it converges to zero as
n\to\infty
. Specifically, note that

N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{c^{\top}\phi_{1}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1})\big\}_{\beta_{1}\in\real^{d_{1}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{1}\|_{1}\leq k},~L_{1+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)<\infty,

for any
c\in\real^{d_{1}}
and any
\epsilon>0
by assumption
(CP3)
. Thus, by the Uniform Weak Law of Large Numbers, we have that

\sup_{\beta_{1}\in\real^{d_{1}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{1}\|_{1}\leq k}\left|c^{\top}\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1}\big)-c^{\top}\Phi_{1}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\right|\overset{P}{\to}0,

for any
c\in\real^{d_{1}}
. Thus, by Cramer Wold device we have that

\sup_{\beta_{1}\in\real^{d_{1}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{1}\|_{1}\leq k}\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1}\big)-\Phi_{1}\big(\beta_{1}\big)\big\|_{1}\overset{P}{\to}0.

Thus, the expression in display (
B.1
) above converges to zero.

Induction Step.

For our induction assumption, we assume that
\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}
. Given this assumption, we will show that
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{t}^{*}
.

We now define the following useful functions:

\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t}\big)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi(\beta_{1:t-1})}\left[\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)\right]

(B.2)

and

\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:t}\big)\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big).

(B.3)

For now, we take as given that display (
B.4
) below holds; we will show this result holds at the end of this proof.

\left\|\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\right\|_{1}=o_{P}(1).

(B.4)

Let
\epsilon>0
. By assumption
(CP1)
, there exists some
\eta>0
such that if
\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}
satisfies
\big\|\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon
, then
\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{t}\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big]\big\|_{1}>\eta>0
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}-\beta_{t}^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta\right)

Note
\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)+\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\\
\leq\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}+\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}+o_{P}(1)
; the last equality holds by display (
B.4
) and the definition of
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}
from display (
3.9
). Thus,

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right)

Note
\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big)\\
=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})=o_{P}(1)
; the second to last equality holds by the definition of
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}
from display (
3.9
). Thus,

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right)

By assumption
(CP2)
, for any
\delta>0
, there exists some
k<\infty
such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
. We use this
k
below:

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\left\{\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}+\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}\right\}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)+o(1)

\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\beta_{t}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\beta_{t}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\bigg)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)+o(1)

(B.5)

Recall
\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}
by our induction assumption; thus,
\mathbb{I}_{\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\in B_{1:t-1}}\overset{P}{\to}1
, where recall that
B_{1:t-1}\subset\real^{d_{1:t-1}}
is a compact subset whose interior contains
\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}
. Thus,

\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\big\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\bigg)}_{=o(1)}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)}_{\leq\delta}+o(1)

Note that the above converges to zero as
n\to\infty
for the following reasons:

•

By assumption
(CP2)
,
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
and
\delta
can be made arbitrarily small.

•

Note that by Cramer Wold device, to show that

\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\left\|\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)\right\|_{1}\overset{P}{\to}0,

it is sufficient to show that for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
,

\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)\right\}\overset{P}{\to}0.

Also, note that

\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{t}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}\big)\right\}

=\sup_{\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\\
-\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\right]\bigg\}\overset{P}{\to}0.

The above convergence result holds by Theorem
D.2
(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Uniform Weak Law of Large Numbers).
Specifically we are able to apply Theorem
D.2
because Condition
2
holds and
N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{\Pi c^{\top}\phi_{t}}(B_{1:t-1},k),L_{1+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)<\infty
for all
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
and all
\epsilon>0
, where

\mathcal{F}_{\Pi c^{\top}\phi_{t}}(B_{1:t-1},k)\triangleq\bigg\{\bigg[\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t^{\prime}-1})\bigg]c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}\big)\bigg\}_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1},~\beta_{t}\in\real^{d_{t}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\beta_{t}\|_{1}\leq k}.

The above finite bracketing number result holds since using assumption
(CP3)
and Condition
3
, we can apply by Lemma
A.5
(specifically see Remark
8
part
(b1)
).

We now show that display (
B.4
) holds.

Let
\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}
.

\left\|\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\right\|_{1}

=\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left\{W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)-W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\big)\right\}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\bigg]\bigg\|_{1}

=\bigg\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\left\{W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)-W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)\right\}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\bigg]\bigg\|_{1}

By Jensen’s inequality,

\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\left|W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1},\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)-W_{2:t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)\right|\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

By definition of
W_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t^{\prime}-1},\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}^{*}\big)
from display (
5.5
),

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg|\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}\big(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}\big)-\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}\big(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}^{*}\big)\bigg|\\
\bigg\{\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\frac{1}{\pi_{t^{\prime}}\big(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}^{*}\big)}\bigg\}\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

By Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration),
\pi_{t^{\prime}}\big(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}^{*}\big)^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{-1}
a.s. Thus,

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg|\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1})-\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1}^{*})\bigg|\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

(B.6)

By Condition
3
, we can apply Lemma
A.4
(Product of Lipschitz Policy Functions are Lipschitz) to get that

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg\{\sum_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\bigg\}\big\|\beta_{1:t-1}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\bigg]

By linearity of expectations,

=\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}\bigg\{\sum_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right]\bigg\}\big\|\beta_{1:t-1}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}.

Thus, by consolidating the above results, we have that

\big\|\Phi_{t}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{t}\big(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\\
\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}\bigg\{\sum_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right]\bigg\}\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}=o_{P}(1).

The last limit above holds because

•

\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}=o_{P}(1)
since
\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}
by our induction assumption.

•

By assumption
(CP3)
(Finite Bracketing Number for Policy Functions), there exists a function
F_{\phi_{t}}
such that
\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)\big\|_{1}\leq F_{\phi_{t}}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
a.s. and for all
t^{\prime}\in[2\colon t]
,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[F_{\phi_{t}}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}\big)\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\big]<\infty
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[F_{\phi_{t}}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}\big)\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right]<\infty.

We have now shown that display (
B.4
) holds.
\blacksquare

B.2
Inequality Using Binomial Theorem (Helper Lemma
B.2
)

Lemma B.2

(Inequality Using Binomial Theorem)
.

For any
\eta\geq 1
and any
a,b\in\real
, we have that
|a+b|^{\eta}\leq c_{\eta}(|a|^{\eta}+|b|^{\eta})
for some constant
c_{\eta}<\infty
.

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

Note that

|a+b|^{\eta}\leq\begin{cases}|a-b|^{\lfloor\eta\rfloor}&\textnormal{ if }|a+b|<1\\
|a-b|^{\lceil\eta\rceil}&\textnormal{ if }|a+b|\geq 1\\
\end{cases}

Above we use
\lfloor\eta\rfloor
to round
\eta
down to the nearest integer and we use
\lceil\eta\rceil
to round
\eta
up to the nearest integer.
Let

k\triangleq\begin{cases}\lfloor\eta\rfloor&\textnormal{ if }|a+b|<1\\
\lceil\eta\rceil&\textnormal{ if }|a+b|\geq 1\end{cases}.

Note
k\geq 1
since that
\lceil\eta\rceil\geq\lfloor\eta\rfloor\geq 1
because
\eta\geq 1
.
Since
k
is a positive integer, by the Binomial theorem,

|a+b|^{k}=\big|(a+b)^{k}\big|=\bigg|\sum_{j=0}^{k}{k\choose j}a^{j}~b^{k-j}\bigg|=\sum_{j=0}^{k}{k\choose j}|a|^{j}~|b|^{k-j}

Note that
|a|^{j}~|b|^{k-j}\leq\max\big(|a|^{k},|b|^{k}\big)
for all
j\in[0\colon k]
. Thus,

\leq\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{k}{k\choose j}\bigg\}\max\big(|a|^{k},|b|^{k}\big)\leq\bigg\{\sum_{j=0}^{k}{k\choose j}\bigg\}\big(|a|^{k}+|b|^{k}\big).

Thus we can choose
c_{\eta}=\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{k}{k\choose j}\right\}
.
\blacksquare

B.3
Asymptotic Equicontinuity for Policy Parameters (Theorem
B.3
)

Theorem B.3

(Asymptotic Equicontinuity for Policy Parameters)
.

We assume that Conditions
1
-
4
on the adaptive sampling algorithm hold. Consider the following results:

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:T-1}(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})\right\}\\
=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})-\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)

(B.7)

and

\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1).

The above results hold under the following additional assumptions:

(NP1)

Finite Bracketing Integral:

Let
\alpha>0
be a constant. For each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
, for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
,

\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t})\big\}_{\beta_{t}\in B_{t}},~L_{2+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)}d\epsilon<\infty.

(B.8)

Additionally, there exists a function
F_{\phi_{t}}
such that for all
\beta_{t}\in B_{t}
,
\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big\|_{1}\leq F_{\phi_{t}}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
a.s. and

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big|F_{\phi,t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\big|^{2+\alpha}\right]<\infty

(B.9)

for all
t^{\prime}\in[2\colon T]
; the functions
\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}
above are from Condition
3
.

(NP2)

Continuity Condition:

For each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
, the following mapping is continuous at
\beta_{t}=\beta_{t}^{*}
:

\beta_{t}\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)-\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\right].

Remark 9

(Condition
5
implies assumptions
(NP1)
and
(NP2)
above hold)
.

As we discussed in the Remark below Condition
5
(Lipschitz Policy Estimating Function), Condition
5
can be replaced by more general assumptions; these are assumptions
(NP1)
and
(NP2)
above.

•

Condition
5
implies assumption
(NP1)
because

–

Example 19.7 of
Van der Vaart 2000
shows that Lipschitz property of Condition
5
and the compactness of
B_{t}
implies that the bracketing integral condition from display (
B.8
) holds.

–

We now show why Condition
5
implies that display (
B.9
) holds. Since
B_{t}
is compact, let
\textnormal{diam}(B_{t})<\infty
be the diameter of
B_{t}
in Euclidian distance. Note the following inequality for all
\beta_{t}\in B_{t}
,

\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{t}}\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big\|_{2}\\
\leq\sqrt{d_{t}}\left\{\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\big\|_{2}+\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\textnormal{diam}(B_{t})\right\}\textnormal{~~a.s.}

The first inequality above holds by property of norms and the second inequality above holds by display (
5.18
) of Condition
3
. Furthermore, note that

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\left|\sqrt{d_{t}}\left\{\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\big\|_{2}+\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\textnormal{diam}(B_{t})\right\}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right|^{2+\alpha}\right].

By Lemma
B.2
(Inequality Using Binomial Theorem), for some positive constant
c_{2+\alpha}<\infty
,

\leq c_{2+\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\left|\sqrt{d_{t}}\big\|\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\big\|_{2}\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right|^{2+\alpha}\right]\\
+c_{2+\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\left|\sqrt{d_{t}}\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\textnormal{diam}(B_{t})\dot{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\right|^{2+\alpha}\right]<\infty.

The final inequality holds by Condition
5
. The above implies that display (
B.9
) holds.

•

We now discuss why Condition
5
implies assumption
(NP2)
holds. Let
\epsilon>0
. We want to show that there exists some
\delta>0
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)-\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon
whenever
\big\|\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\leq\delta
.
By Condition
5
, for any
\beta_{t}\in B_{t}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\|\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)-\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})^{2}\big]\big\|\beta_{t}-\beta_{t}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}.

Recall
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})^{2}\big]<\infty
by Condition
5
. Thus,
\delta=\epsilon^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\dot{\phi}_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})^{2}\big]^{-1/2}
is sufficient.

Proof of Theorem
B.3
.

Recall in displays (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), we defined the functions
\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1})
and
\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:T-1})
respectively. More generally, we now define for
any

t\in[1\colon T-1]
the following functions:

\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t})\triangleq\mathbb{E}\begin{bmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1})\\
W_{2}^{(i)}(\beta_{1},\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2})\\
W_{2:3}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:2},\hat{\beta}_{1:2}^{(n)})\phi_{3}(\mathcal{H}_{3}^{(i)};\beta_{3})\\
\vdots\\
W_{2:t}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)})\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\end{bmatrix}

and

\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t})\triangleq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1})\\
W_{2}^{(i)}(\beta_{1},\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2})\\
W_{2:3}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:2},\hat{\beta}_{1:2}^{(n)})\phi_{3}(\mathcal{H}_{3}^{(i)};\beta_{3})\\
\vdots\\
W_{2:t}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:t-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)})\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\end{bmatrix}.

We use an induction argument.

•

For the base case,
t=1
, we will show that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
and that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

•

For the induction step,
t>1
, we assume that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
and will show that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
and that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

To show that the Theorem holds, it is sufficient to show the above results hold.

Base Case.

Note that since
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(2)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(3)},\dots,\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(n)}
are i.i.d., we can use an asymptotic normality argument for Z-estimators on i.i.d. data.

Stochastic Equicontinuity Result.

First, we will apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) to get that for any fixed vector
c\in\real^{d_{1}}
,

\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

(B.10)

We are able to apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) because the following assumptions hold:

•

Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Functions) hold.

•

By assumption
(NP1)
(Finite Bracketing Integral), for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{1}}
,

\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{c^{\top}\phi_{1}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1})\big\}_{\beta_{1}\in B_{1}},~L_{2+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)}d\epsilon<\infty.

•

\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1}^{*}
by Condition
1
. Thus, by assumption
(NP2)
and continuous mapping theorem we have that for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{1}}
,
\nu\big(c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}),~c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}^{*})\big)\overset{P}{\to}0
, where

\nu\bigg(c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}),~c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}^{\prime})\bigg)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\left\{c^{\top}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}\big)-c^{\top}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{\prime}\big)\right\}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

By display (
B.10
) and Cramer Wold device, we have that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

(B.11)

With the above result, for the base case of the induction argument, we now just need to show that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
.

Showing that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
.

By the definitions of
\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}
and
\beta_{1}^{*}
from displays (
3.9
) and (
3.7
) respectively, we can rewrite the left-hand side of the display (
B.11
) above:

\sqrt{n}\big\{\underbrace{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})}_{=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})}-\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\big\}=\sqrt{n}\big\{\underbrace{\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})}_{=0}-\Phi_{1}(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)})\big\}+o_{P}(1).

By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions), the mapping
\beta_{1}\mapsto\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1})
is differentiable at
\beta_{1}=\beta_{1}^{*}
with derivative matrix
\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1}}}\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1})\big|_{\beta_{1}=\beta_{1}^{*}}
. So,

=-\sqrt{n}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)+o_{P}(1).

In summary, we have that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)=-\sqrt{n}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big).

By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions),
\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}
is invertible, so
\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}=O(1)
and

\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)\\
=-\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big).

(B.12)

By the central limit theorem (for i.i.d. data),

\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}\big\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\underbrace{\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})}_{=0}\big\}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}^{*}\big)+o_{P}(1)\\
\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,~\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}\Sigma_{1}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1,\top}\right),

(B.13)

where
\Sigma_{1}=\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}^{*}\big)^{\otimes 2}\big]
.

By displays (
B.12
) and (
B.13
) we have that
-\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)=O_{P}(1)
. This implies that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
; thus,
\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)=o_{P}(1)
. Using these results, we have that

\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1}^{*}\big)=-\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1}^{*})-\Phi_{1}(\beta_{1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)=O_{P}(1).

Induction Step.

For the induction step, for a given
t>1
, we make the induction assumption that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
. We then show that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
and
\sqrt{n}\big\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\big\}=\sqrt{n}\big\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\big\}+o_{P}(1)
.

Stochastic Equicontinuity Result.

First, we will apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) to get that for any fixed vector
c\in\real^{d_{1:t}}
(we use
d_{1:t}\triangleq\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}d_{t^{\prime}}
),

\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

(B.14)

We are able to apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) because the following assumptions hold:

•

Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Functions) hold.

•

\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t-1}^{*}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
by our induction assumption.

•

Since assumption
(NP1)
(Finite Bracketing Integral) and Condition
3
(Lipschitz Policy Function) hold, we can apply Lemma
A.5
(Finite Bracketing Integral) to get that for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
,

\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t})\big\}_{\beta_{1:t}\in B_{1:t}},~L_{2+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)}d\epsilon<\infty,

where
\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1})\triangleq\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t^{\prime}-1})
; specifically see the result in Remark
8
part
(b2)
below the statement of Lemma
A.5
.

•

\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:t}^{*}
by Condition
1
. Thus, by assumption
(NP2)
and continuous mapping theorem we have that for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{t}}
,

\nu\left(\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}),~\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1}^{*})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}^{*})\right)\overset{P}{\to}0
, where

\nu\bigg(\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}),~\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1}^{\prime})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}^{\prime})\bigg)\\
\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\left\{\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t})-\pi_{2:t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:t-1}^{\prime})c^{\top}\phi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t}^{\prime})\right\}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

By display (
B.14
) and Cramer Wold device, we have that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\right\}=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

(B.15)

With the above result, for the induction step of the induction argument, we now just need to show that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
.

Showing that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
.

By the definitions of
\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}
and
\beta_{1:t}^{*}
from displays (
3.9
) and (
3.7
) respectively, we can rewrite the left-hand side of the display (
B.15
) above:

\sqrt{n}\big[\underbrace{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})}_{=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})}-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\big]=\sqrt{n}\big[\underbrace{\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})}_{=0}-\Phi_{1:t}(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)})\big]+o_{P}(1).

By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions), the mapping
\beta_{1:t}\mapsto\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t})
is differentiable at
\beta_{1:t}=\beta_{1:t}^{*}
with the derivative matrix

\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1:t}}}\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t})\big|_{\beta_{1:t}=\beta_{1:t}^{*}}
. So,

=-\sqrt{n}\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)+o_{P}(1).

In summary, we have that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)=-\sqrt{n}\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big).

By Condition
4
, we can apply Lemma
B.4
(Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
) to get that
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible; so
\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}=O(1)
and

\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)\\
=-\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big).

(B.16)

For now, we take as given that the following result in display (
B.17
) holds; we prove this at the end of this proof.

\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}=O_{P}(1).

(B.17)

By displays (
B.16
) and (
B.17
) we have that
-\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)=O_{P}(1)
. This implies that
\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=O_{P}(1)
; thus,
\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big\|_{2}\big)=o_{P}(1)
. Using these results, we have that

\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:t}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:t}^{*}\big)=-\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1)=O_{P}(1).

We now show that display (
B.17
) holds..

For any fixed vector
c=[c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{t}]\in\real^{d_{1:t}}
,

\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\big\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\underbrace{\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})}_{=0}\big\}\\
=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{t}c_{k}^{\top}W_{2:k}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:k-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{1:k-1}^{(n)})\phi_{k}(\mathcal{H}_{k}^{(i)};\beta_{k}^{*})\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,c^{\top}\Sigma_{1:t}c\right),

(B.18)

where

\Sigma_{1:t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2}^{*}}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}^{*})\\
\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2}^{*})\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\end{pmatrix}^{\otimes 2}\right].

The asymptotic normality result above holds by Theorem
D.4
(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Central Limit Theorem). Specifically we can apply Theorem
D.4
because:

•

Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Functions) hold.

•

By our induction assumption,
\hat{\beta}_{t^{\prime}}^{(n)}-\beta_{t^{\prime}}^{*}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
for all
t^{\prime}\in[1\colon t-1]
.

•

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\big|\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\phi_{t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}}^{*})\big|^{2+\alpha}\big]<\infty
by the Finite Bracketing Integral assumption
(NP1)
.

Thus, by Cramer-Wold device and display (
B.18
) we have that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{1:t}).

By Condition
4
, we can apply Lemma
B.4
(Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
) to get that
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible. Thus, by continuous mapping theorem,

\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\right\}\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\Sigma_{1:t}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1,\top}\right).

The above implies that display (
B.17
) holds, i.e., that
\sqrt{n}\big[\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\big]^{-1}\big\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:t}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})-\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t}^{*})\big\}=O_{P}(1)
.
\blacksquare

B.4
Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
(Lemma
B.4
)

Lemma B.4

(Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
)
.

Under Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions), for each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
,
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible.

In the proof of Lemma
B.4
we will use the following proposition:

Proposition 1

(Blockwise Inversion of Matrix)
.

Let
A\in\real^{k\times k}
,
B\in\real^{k\times j}
,
C\in\real^{j\times k}
, and
D\in\real^{j\times j}
. If
A
and
D-CA^{-1}B
are invertible then

\begin{bmatrix}A&&B\\
C&&D\end{bmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{-1}+A^{-1}B(D-CA^{-1}B)^{-1}CA^{-1}&&-A^{-1}B(D-CA^{-1}B)^{-1}\\
-(D-CA^{-1}B)^{-1}CA^{-1}&&(D-CA^{-1}B)^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Furthermore, in the special case that
B=0
,

\begin{bmatrix}A&&0\\
C&&D\end{bmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{-1}&&0\\
-D^{-1}CA^{-1}&&D^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

This is a result is proved in Proposition 3.9.7 of
Bernstein 2018
.

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions), the mapping
\beta_{1:t}\mapsto\Phi_{1:t}(\beta_{1:t})
is differentiable at
\beta_{1:t}=\beta_{1:t}^{*}
.
Let
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1:t}}}\Phi_{t}(\beta_{1:t})\big|_{\beta_{1:t}=\beta_{1:t}^{*}}
Specifically,

\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}=\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1:t}}}\mathbb{E}_{\pi(\beta_{1:t-1})}\begin{bmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1})\\
\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2})\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\end{bmatrix}\bigg|_{\beta_{1:t}=\beta_{1:t}^{*}}=\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}&&0&&0&&\ldots&&0\\
V_{2,1}&&\dot{\Phi}_{2}^{*}&&0&&\ldots&&0\\
V_{3,1}&&V_{3,2}&&\dot{\Phi}_{3}^{*}&&\ldots&&0\\
\vdots&&\vdots&&\vdots&&\ddots&&\vdots\\
V_{t,1}&&V_{t,2}&&V_{t,3}&&\ldots&&\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}\end{bmatrix},

where
V_{t,s}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{s}}}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})W_{s+1}^{(i)}(\beta_{s},\beta_{s}^{*})\right]\big|_{\beta_{s}=\beta_{s}^{*}}\in\real^{d_{t}\times d_{s}}
and

\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{t}}}\Phi_{t}(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\beta_{t})\big|_{\beta_{t}=\beta_{t}^{*}}\triangleq\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{t}}}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t})\big]\big|_{\beta_{t}=\beta_{t}^{*}}
.

Note that by repeatedly applying Proposition
1
we can show that
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible. Moreover, we will show that the inverse of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is lower block triangular. To see this, consider the following induction argument:

•

Base case:
By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions),
\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}
and
\dot{\Phi}_{2}^{*}
are both invertible. Thus, by Proposition
1
, the matrix
\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}&0\\
V_{2,1}&\dot{\Phi}_{2}^{*}\end{bmatrix}
is invertible; moreover, its inverse is lower block triangular.

•

Induction step:
Suppose we know that for some
t\geq 2
, the inverse of the following lower block triangular matrix is invertible:

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}&0&0&\ldots&0\\
V_{2,1}&\dot{\Phi}_{2}^{*}&0&\ldots&0\\
V_{3,1}&V_{3,2}&\dot{\Phi}_{3}^{*}&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
V_{t-1,1}&V_{t-1,2}&V_{t-1,3}&\ldots&\dot{\Phi}_{t-1}^{*}\end{bmatrix}.

(B.19)

By Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions),
\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}
is invertible. By Proposition
1
we can conclude that the following matrix is invertible and lower block triangular:

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1}^{*}&0&0&\ldots&0&0\\
V_{2,1}&\dot{\Phi}_{2}^{*}&0&\ldots&0&0\\
V_{3,1}&V_{3,2}&\dot{\Phi}_{3}^{*}&\ldots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
V_{t-1,1}&V_{t-1,2}&V_{t-1,3}&\ldots&\dot{\Phi}_{t-1}^{*}&0\\
V_{t,1}&V_{t,2}&V_{t,3}&\ldots&V_{t,t-1}&\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}\end{bmatrix}.

When applying Proposition
1
, take the matrix from display (
B.19
) (upper left-hand side of the matrix above) to be matrix
A
; take
\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}
to be matrix
D
; take
\begin{bmatrix}V_{t,1}&V_{t,2}&V_{t,3}&\ldots&V_{t,t-1}\end{bmatrix}
to be matrix
C
; and take the block of zeros above
\dot{\Phi}_{t}^{*}
to be matrix
B
.

By the above argument,
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible for any
t\in[1\colon T-1]
.
~\blacksquare

Appendix C
Main Asymptotic Results

Overview of Supplement
C
Results.

•

Section
C.1
:
Consistency of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
(Theorem
5.1
)

•

Section
C.2
:
Equivalent Formulations for the Adaptive Sandwich Variance (Lemma
C.1
)

•

Section
C.3
:
Asymptotic Normality of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
(Theorem
5.2
)

C.1
Consistency of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
(Theorem
5.1
)

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Note that this argument is extremely similar to the proof for Theorem
B.1
. The proof will use the estimating functions
\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)
and
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)
defined earlier in displays (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) respectively.

For now, we take as given that display (
B.4
) below holds; we will show this result holds at the end of this proof.

\left\|\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\right\|_{1}=o_{P}(1).

(C.1)

Let
\epsilon>0
. By assumption
(C1)
, there exists some
\eta>0
such that if
\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}
satisfies
\big\|\theta-\theta^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon
, then
\big\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\big[\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta)\big]\big\|_{1}>\eta>0
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\big\|_{1}>\epsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta\right).

Note
\big\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}=\big\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)+\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\\
\leq\big\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}+\big\|\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\\
=\big\|\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}+o_{P}(1)
; the last equality holds by display (
C.1
). Thus,

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right).

Note that
\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{2:T}^{(i)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\\
=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})=o_{P}(1)
; the second to last equality holds by the definition of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
from display (
3.2
). Thus,

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right).

By assumption
(C2)
, for any
\delta>0
, there exists some
k<\infty
such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
. We use this
k
below:

=\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}\left\{\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}+\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}\right\}>\eta-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\|_{1}>k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)

\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\big\|_{1}~\mathbb{I}_{\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\|_{1}\leq k}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\right)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)+o(1)

\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\theta\big)-\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\theta\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\bigg)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)+o(1)

Since
\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}
by Condition
1
, thus
\mathbb{I}_{\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\in B_{1:T-1}}\overset{P}{\to}1
; recall that
B_{1:T-1}\subset\real^{d_{1:T-1}}
is a compact subset whose interior contains
\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}
. Thus,

\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\big\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)\big\|_{1}>\eta/2-o_{P}(1)\bigg)}_{=o(1)}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\left(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\right)}_{\leq\delta}+o(1).

Note that the above converges to zero as
n\to\infty
for the following reasons:

•

By assumption
(CP2)
,
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\big\|\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big\|_{1}>k\big)\leq\delta
and
\delta
can be made arbitrarily small.

•

Note that by Cramer Wold device, to show that

\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\left\|\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)\right\|_{1}\overset{P}{\to}0,

it is sufficient to show that for any vector
c\in\real^{d_{\theta}}
,

\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)\right\}\overset{P}{\to}0.

Also note that

\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}c^{\top}\left\{\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)\right\}

=\sup_{\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}~\sup_{\beta_{1:t-1}\in B_{1:t-1}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{W_{2:T}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:T-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})c^{\top}\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta)\\
-\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2:T}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:T-1},\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})c^{\top}\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta)\right]\bigg\}\overset{P}{\to}0.

The above convergence result holds by Theorem
D.2
(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Uniform Weak Law of Large Numbers).
Specifically we are able to apply Theorem
D.2
because Condition
2
holds and
N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{\Pi c^{\top}\psi}(B_{1:T-1},k),L_{1+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)<\infty
for any
c\in\real^{d_{\theta}}
and any
\epsilon>0
, where

\mathcal{F}_{\Pi c^{\top}\psi}(B_{1:T-1},k)\triangleq\bigg\{\bigg[\prod_{t=2}^{T}\pi_{t}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t-1})\bigg]c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta\big)\bigg\}_{\beta_{1:T-1}\in B_{1:T-1},~\theta\in\real^{d_{\theta}}\textnormal{~s.t.~}\|\theta\|_{1}\leq k}.

The above finite bracketing number result holds since using assumption
(CP3)
and Condition
3
, we can apply by Lemma
A.5
(specifically see Remark
8
part
(a1)
).

We now show that display (
C.1
) holds.

Let
\beta_{1:T-1}\in B_{1:T-1}
.

\left\|\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\right\|_{1}

=\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\{\prod_{t=2}^{T}W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1},\hat{\beta}_{t-1}^{(n)}\big)-W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t-1}^{(n)}\big)\bigg\}\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\bigg]\bigg\|_{1}

=\bigg\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\{\prod_{t=2}^{T}W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1},\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)-W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1}^{*},\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)\bigg\}\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\bigg]\bigg\|_{1}

By Jensen’s inequality,

\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg|\prod_{t=2}^{T}W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1},\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)-W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1}^{*},\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)\bigg|\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

By definition of
W_{t}^{(i)}\big(\beta_{t-1},\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)
from display (
5.5
),

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg|\prod_{t=2}^{T}\pi_{t}\big(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t-1}\big)-\prod_{t=2}^{T}\pi_{t}\big(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)\bigg|\\
\bigg\{\prod_{t=2}^{T}\frac{1}{\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})}\bigg\}\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

By Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration),
\pi_{t}^{*}\big(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{-1}
a.s. Thus,

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg|\prod_{t=2}^{T}\pi_{t}\big(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t-1}\big)-\prod_{t=2}^{T}\pi_{t}\big(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t-1}^{*}\big)\bigg|\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg]

By Condition
3
and Lemma
A.4
(Product of Lipschitz Policy Functions are Lipschitz),

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\bigg\{\sum_{t=2}^{T}\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\bigg\}\big\|\beta_{1:T-1}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}\bigg]

By linearity of expectations,

=\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\bigg\{\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\right]\bigg\}\big\|\beta_{1:T-1}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}.

Thus, by consolidating the above results, we have that

\big\|\Psi{}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi{}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\\
\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\bigg\{\sum_{t=2}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\right]\bigg\}\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}=o_{P}(1).

The last limit above holds because

•

\big\|\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\|_{2}=o_{P}(1)
since
\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}
by Condition
1
.

•

By assumption
(C3)
(Finite Bracketing Number for Policy Functions), there exists a function
F_{\psi}
such that
\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\leq F_{\psi}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
a.s. and for all
t\in[2\colon T]
,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\big[F_{\psi}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)}\big)\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\big]<\infty
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\|\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)\big\|_{1}\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[F_{\psi}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)}\big)\dot{\pi}_{t}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})\right]<\infty.

We have now shown that display (
C.1
) holds.
\blacksquare

C.2
Equivalent Formulations for the Adaptive Sandwich Variance (Lemma
C.1
)

Lemma C.1

(Equivalent Formulations for the Adaptive Sandwich Variance)
.

Let Condition
4
(Differentiability of Policy Parameter Estimating Functions) and assumptions
(N1)
and
(N2)
from Theorem
5.2
(Asymptotic Normality hold). Also let
\Sigma_{1:T}
as defined in display (
5.11
) be finite.

Then, the lower-right
d_{\theta}\times d_{\theta}
block of limiting variance from display (
5.10
), i.e.,

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1,\top},

(C.2)

equals the adaptive sandwich variance, i.e.,
[\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1}\Sigma^{\mathrm{adapt}}[\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1,\top}
from display (
5.3
).

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

By the definition of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{adapt}}
from display (
5.3
), it is sufficient to show that the lower-right
d_{\theta}\times d_{\theta}
block of the the limiting variance from display (
C.2
) above equals the following

[\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1}\Sigma^{\mathrm{adapt}}[\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1,\top}\\
=[\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\{\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)+\dot{\Psi}^{*}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}M_{t}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\bigg\}^{\otimes 2}\bigg][\dot{\Psi}^{*}]^{-1,\top}.

Consider the following matrix from display (
C.2
) (the terms in the matrix below are derivatives that exist by Condition
4
, and assumptions
(N1)
and
(N2)
):

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}.

(C.3)

By Proposition
1
(Blockwise Inversion of Matrix), we have that for a block matrix
\begin{bmatrix}A&&0\\
C&&D\end{bmatrix}
, if square matrices
A
and
D
are invertible, then the whole matrix is invertible and

\begin{bmatrix}A&&0\\
C&&D\end{bmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{-1}&&0\\
-D^{-1}CA^{-1}&&D^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

By Condition
4
and Lemma
B.4
(Invertibility of
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
),
\dot{\Phi}_{1:t}^{*}
is invertible and
\dot{\Psi}^{*}
is invertible by assumption
(N1)
. Thus the matrix from display (
C.3
) is invertible and

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}&&\bm{0}\\
-\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}&&\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Recall in display (
5.32
) we defined the following matrices:

M_{1:T-1}\triangleq\big[M_{1},M_{2},\dots,M_{T-1}\big]\triangleq-\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\in\real^{d_{\theta}\times d_{1:T-1}}.

(C.4)

Above we use
d_{1:T-1}\triangleq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}d_{t}
. Thus,

\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1,\top}

=\begin{bmatrix}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}&&\bm{0}\\
M_{1:T-1}&&\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma_{1:T-1}&U_{1:T-1,T}\\
U_{T,1:T-1}&\Sigma\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}&&\bm{0}\\
M_{1:T-1}&&\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}.

(C.5)

Above
\Sigma\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T-1}^{*}}\left[\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*})^{\otimes 2}\right]
,
\Sigma_{1:T-1}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T-1}^{*}}\left[\phi_{1:T-1}(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})^{\otimes 2}\right]
,

U_{T,1:T-1}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*})\phi_{1:T-1}(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})^{\top}\right]
, and
U_{1:T-1,T}\triangleq U_{T,1:T-1}^{\top}
, where

\phi_{1:T-1}(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}^{*})\\
\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2}^{*})\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{T-1}(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{T-1}^{*})\end{pmatrix}.

(C.6)

Thus display (
C.5
) equals the following:

=\begin{bmatrix}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T-1}&&~\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1}U_{1:T-1,T}\\
M_{1:T-1}\Sigma_{1:T-1}+\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}U_{T,1:T-1}&&~M_{1:T-1}U_{1:T-1,T}+\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\Sigma\end{bmatrix}\\
\begin{bmatrix}\big\{\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}&&M_{1:T-1}^{\top}\\
\bm{0}&&\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}\end{bmatrix}

Thus, the lower-right
d_{\theta}\times d_{\theta}
block of the product of matrices above equals the following:

M_{1:T-1}\Sigma_{1:T-1}M_{1:T-1}^{\top}+\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}U_{T,1:T-1}M_{1:T-1}^{\top}\\
+M_{1:T-1}U_{1:T-1,T}\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}+\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\Sigma\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}

=\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\bigg[\dot{\Psi}^{*}M_{1:T-1}\Sigma_{1:T-1}M_{1:T-1}^{\top}\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\}^{\top}+U_{T,1:T-1}M_{1:T-1}^{\top}\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\}^{\top}\\
+\dot{\Psi}^{*}M_{1:T-1}U_{1:T-1,T}+\Sigma\bigg]\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}

=\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\{\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)+\dot{\Psi}^{*}M_{1:T-1}\phi_{1:T-1}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)\bigg\}^{\otimes 2}\bigg]\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}

By the definition of
M_{1:T-1}
from display (
C.4
) and the definition of
\phi_{1:T-1}
from display (
C.6
),

=\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\{\psi\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*}\big)+\dot{\Psi}^{*}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}M_{t}\phi_{t}\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*}\big)\bigg\}^{\otimes 2}\bigg]\big\{\dot{\Psi}^{*}\big\}^{-1,\top}.

We have now shown the desired result.
\blacksquare

C.3
Asymptotic Normality of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
(Theorem
5.2
)

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

By Lemma
C.1
(Equivalent Formulations for the Adaptive Sandwich Variance) above, it is sufficient to show that

\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{pmatrix}\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1,\top}\right).

This proof will use the estimating functions
\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)
,
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)
,

\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1})
, and
\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:T-1})
defined earlier in displays (
5.6
), (
5.7
), (
5.8
), and (
5.9
) respectively.

We now state several equalities and discuss why they hold below:

-\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)\\
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\smash{\underbrace{\phantom{\begin{matrix}\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\end{matrix}}}_{\text{$=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})$}}}$}\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\end{bmatrix}\\
~~\\
\underbrace{=}_{(a)}-\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)\\
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\smash{\underbrace{\phantom{\begin{matrix}\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\end{matrix}}}_{\text{$=0$}}}$}\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\end{bmatrix}+o_{P}(1)\\
~~\\
\underbrace{=}_{(b)}\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\left(\left\|\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{matrix}\right\|_{2}\right)+o_{P}(1).

(C.7)

Equality (a).

Equality (a) above holds since
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
and
\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)=0
by the definitions of
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}
and
\theta^{*}
from displays (
3.2
) and (
3.1
) respectively;
also since
\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
and
\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)=0
by the definitions of
\hat{\beta}_{t}^{(n)}
and
\beta_{t}^{*}
from displays (
3.9
) and (
3.7
).

Equality (b).

Equality (b) above holds by a Taylor series expansion. Specifically by assumptions
(N1)
and
(N2)
, the mapping
\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)\mapsto\Psi(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta)
is differentiable at
\big(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta\big)=\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)
. Additionally the mapping
\beta_{1:T-1}\mapsto\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1})
is differentiable at
\beta_{1:T-1}=\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}
by Condition
4
. Thus,

\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta)}}\begin{bmatrix}\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1})\\
\Psi{}(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta)\end{bmatrix}\bigg|_{(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta)=(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*})}\\
=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1:T-1}}}\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1})\big|_{\beta_{1:T-1}=\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}}&&\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\theta}}\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\big|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}\\
\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\beta_{1:T-1}}}\Psi{}(\beta_{1:T-1},\theta^{*})\big|_{\beta_{1:T-1}=\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}}&&\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\theta}}\Psi{}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta)\big|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}.

As mentioned below display (
5.12
),
\frac{\partial{}}{\partial{\theta}}\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\big|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}=\bm{0}
since
\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})
is not a function of
\theta
.

We now state the next set of results and discuss why they hold below:

-\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big)\\
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\end{bmatrix}\\
\underbrace{=}_{(c)}-\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)\\
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\end{bmatrix}+o_{P}(1)\underbrace{\overset{D}{\to}}_{(d)}\mathcal{N}\big(0,\Sigma_{1:T}\big).

(C.8)

Equality (c).

Equality (c) above is an asymptotic equicontinuity result. We now discuss why equality (c) holds. First note that by Conditions
1
-
5
and Remark
9
(which shows that Condition
5
implies that assumptions
(NP1)
and
(NP2)
hold), we can apply Theorem
B.3
(Asymptotic Equicontinuity for Policy Parameters) to get the following results:

\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})

(C.9)

and

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})-\Phi_{1:T-1}(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})\right\}\\
=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})-\Phi_{1:T-1}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\right\}+o_{P}(1).

(C.10)

We now apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) to get that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)-\Psi\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\right\}\\
=\sqrt{n}\left\{\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\right\}+o_{P}(1),

(C.11)

We are able to apply Lemma
D.6
(Stochastic Equicontinuity) because the following assumptions hold:

•

Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Functions) hold.

•

Note that
\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
by display (
C.9
).

•

Since assumption
(N4)
(Finite Bracketing Integral) and Condition
3
(Lipschitz Policy Function) hold, we can apply Lemma
A.5
(specifically see Remark
8
part
(a2)
) to get that for any
c\in\real^{d_{\theta}}
,

\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\left(\epsilon,~\big\{\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1})c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta)\big\}_{\beta_{1:T-1}\in B_{1:T-1},\theta\in\Theta},~L_{2+\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\right)}d\epsilon<\infty,

where
\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1})\triangleq\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{T}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{t^{\prime}-1})
.

•

\big(\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)},\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\big)\overset{P}{\to}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)
by Slutsky’s theorem since
\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}
by Condition
1
and since
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}\overset{P}{\to}\theta^{*}
by assumption. Thus, by assumption
(N3)
and continuous mapping theorem we have that for any
c\in\real^{d_{\theta}}
,

\nu\left(c^{\top}\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})\psi(\,\cdot\,;\hat{\theta}^{(n)}),~c^{\top}\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1}^{*})\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta^{*})\right)\overset{P}{\to}0
, where

\nu\bigg(\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1})c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta),~\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1}^{\prime})c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta^{\prime})\bigg)\\
\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1})c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta)-\pi_{2:T}(\,\cdot\,;\beta_{1:T-1}^{\prime})c^{\top}\psi(\,\cdot\,;\theta^{\prime})\big\}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Equality (c) holds since by Slutsky’s Theorem, we can combine the results above from displays (
C.10
) and (
C.11
).

Asymptotic normality result (d).

Equality (d) above holds by Theorem
D.4
(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Central Limit Theorem). Specifically note that for any fixed vector
c=[c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{T}]\in\real^{d_{1:T-1}+d_{\theta}}
,

-\sqrt{n}c^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)\\
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\end{bmatrix}

=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}c_{t}^{\top}W_{2:t}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)})\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\\
-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{T}^{\top}W_{2:T}^{(i)}(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)})\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*})\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\big(0,c^{\top}\Sigma_{1:T}c\big)

(C.12)

where

\Sigma_{1:T}\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)};\beta_{1}^{*})\\
\phi_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{2}^{(i)};\beta_{2}^{*})\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{T-1}(\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)};\beta_{T-1}^{*})\\
\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*})\end{pmatrix}^{\otimes 2}\right].

The final asymptotic normality result above holds by Theorem
D.4
(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Central Limit Theorem). Specifically we can apply Theorem
D.4
because:

•

Conditions
2
(Minimum Exploration) and
3
(Lipschitz Policy Functions) hold.

•

\hat{\beta}_{t^{\prime}}^{(n)}-\beta_{t^{\prime}}^{*}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
for all
t^{\prime}\in[1\colon T-1]
by display (
C.9
).

•

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big|c_{T}^{\top}\psi(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)};\theta^{*})\big|^{2+\alpha}\right]<\infty
by Finite Bracketing Integral assumption
(N4)
. Also,
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big|c_{t}^{\top}\phi_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};\beta_{t}^{*})\big|^{2+\alpha}\right]<\infty
for each
t\in[1\colon T-1]
by Condition
5
.

Thus, by Cramer-Wold device and display (
C.12
) we have that

-\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)-\Phi_{1:T-1}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\big)\\
\hat{\Psi}^{(n)}\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)-\Psi\big(\beta_{1:T-1}^{*},\theta^{*}\big)\end{bmatrix}\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{1:T}).

(C.13)

Thus, we have shown that asymptotic normality result (d) holds.

Consolidating Results.

By consolidating the results from displays (
C.7
) and (
C.8
) above, and applying Slutsky’s theorem we get the following result:

\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}+\sqrt{n}o_{P}\left(\left\|\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{matrix}\right\|_{2}\right)\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\big(0,\Sigma_{1:T}\big).

(C.14)

Note that
\dot{\Psi}^{*}
is invertible by assumption
(N1)
and
\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}
is invertible by Condition
4
and Lemma
B.4
. By Proposition
1
(Block Inversion of Matrices), this is sufficient for
\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}
to be invertible.
Thus, by continuous mapping theorem, and display (
C.14
),

\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\left(\left\|\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{matrix}\right\|_{2}\right)+o_{P}(1)\\
\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1,\top}\right).

(C.15)

The asymptotic normality result above in display (
C.15
) implies that

\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}+\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\left(\left\|\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{matrix}\right\|_{2}\right)=O_{P}(1).

This implies that
\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}=O_{P}(1)
.
Thus, we have that

\sqrt{n}O(1)o_{P}\left(\left\|\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{matrix}\right\|_{2}\right)=o_{P}(1).

So, by display (
C.15
) and Slutsky’s Theorem we have that

\sqrt{n}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\beta}_{1:T-1}^{(n)}-\beta_{1:T-1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}^{(n)}-\theta^{*}\end{bmatrix}\overset{D}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1}\Sigma_{1:T}\begin{bmatrix}\dot{\Phi}_{1:T-1}^{*}&&\bm{0}\\
\bm{V}_{T,1:T-1}&&\dot{\Psi}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{-1,\top}\right).~\blacksquare

D.1
Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Law of Large Numbers (Theorem
D.1
)

Theorem D.1

(Weighted Martingale Triangular Array Weak Law of Large Numbers)
.

Let
f
be a real-valued function of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
such that for some
\alpha>0
,
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big|f\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}\big)\big|^{1+\alpha}\right]<\infty
. Under Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration),

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{2:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\overset{P}{\to}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right].

(D.1)

Moreover,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\overset{P}{\to}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right].

(D.2)

Proof of Theorem
D.1
.

Proving display (
D.1
) holds.

To show display (
D.1
) holds it is sufficient to show that for any
t\in[1\colon T]
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right\}\overset{P}{\to}0.

(D.3)

Above we use
W_{1}^{(i)}=1
and
W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})\triangleq W_{2:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})
.

For the
t=1
case,
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(2)},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(3)},\dots,\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(n)}
are i.i.d.; in this case, display (
D.3
) holds by the Weak Law of Large numbers for i.i.d. data.

Note that for
t\geq 2
, the distribution of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
is changing with the number of users
n
, since it is depends on policy parameter
\hat{\beta}_{t-1}^{(n)}
. Thus, we need to consider triangular array asymptotics.

The first task is to rewrite the left-hand side of display (
D.3
) as a sum of triangular array martingale differences.
Note that we can rewrite the left-hand side of display (
D.3
) as follows:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right\}

Above the expectation
\mathbb{E}
is with respect to the data distribution used to collect the data, thus,
\mathbb{E}\left[W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]
.

Let
X_{t}^{(i)}\triangleq W_{1:t}^{(i)}(\beta^{*},\hat{\beta}^{(n)})f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
. Also let
\mathcal{H}_{0}^{(1:n)}\triangleq\emptyset
and
S_{T+1}^{(1:n)}\triangleq\emptyset
(the second definition is only used for the
t=T
case). By telescoping series,

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{X_{t}^{(i)}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\right]\right\}

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{(1:n)},S_{1}^{(1:n)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\right]\right\}}_{\triangleq Z_{0}^{(i)}}\\
+\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underbrace{\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]\right\}}_{\triangleq Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}}\bigg].

(D.4)

Note above that
X_{t}^{(i)}=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(1:n)},S_{t+1}^{(1:n)}\right]
, since
X_{t}^{(i)}
is a constant given
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(1:n)}
.

Using the
Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}
notation defined in display (
D.4
) above,

=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{0}^{(i)}}_{=o_{P}(1)}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}

(D.5)

Note above that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{0}^{(i)}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{(1:n)},S_{1}^{(1:n)}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big]\right\}\\
=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\big[f(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)})\big|S_{1}^{(1:n)}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[f(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)})\big]\right\}\\
=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\big[f(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)})\big|S_{1}^{(i)}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[f(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)})\big]\right\}\overset{P}{\to}0
by the weak law of large numbers for i.i.d. random variables.

Regarding the second summation in display (
D.5
), note that
\big\{Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big\}_{i=1;t^{\prime}=1}^{i=n;t^{\prime}=t}
is a martingale difference triangular array with respect to the filtration
\big\{\sigma\big(\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\big)\big\}_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}
. This is the case because for any
i\in[1\colon n]
and
t^{\prime}\in[0\colon t]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]

(D.6)

=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\right]\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]=0.

The final equality above holds by the law of iterated expectations.

The next step will be to apply Theorem 2(a) of
Andrews 1988
(Weak Law of Large Numbers for Triangular Array Mixingales). Theorem 2(a) ensures that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\overset{P}{\to}0
if we can show the following hold:

(i)

\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\big]=0
for all
i\in[1\colon n]
and
t^{\prime}\in[1\colon t]
. (Note that since we have a martingale difference triangular array, the mixing constant
c_{n,i}
in Theorem 2(a) is satisfied for
c_{n,i}=0
.)

(ii)

For some
\alpha>0
,
\mathbb{E}\big[|Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}|^{1+\alpha}\big]<\infty
for all
i\in[1\colon n]
and
t^{\prime}\in[1\colon t]
. Note that by Exercise 5.5.1 of
Durrett 2019
this implies
Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}
are uniformly integrable.

We already showed that property
(i)
above holds earlier in display (
D.6
). All that remains is to show that property
(ii)
above holds.

Consider any
t^{\prime}\in[1\colon t]
and any
i\in[1\colon n]
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\big|Z_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}\big|^{1+\alpha}\big]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\big]\right|^{1+\alpha}\bigg]

By Lemma
B.2
, for some positive constant
c_{1+\alpha}<\infty
,

\leq c_{1+\alpha}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\big]\right|^{1+\alpha}\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|\mathbb{E}\big[X_{t}^{(i)}\big|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\big]\right|^{1+\alpha}\bigg]\bigg\}

By Jensen’s inequality

\leq c_{1+\alpha}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\left[\big|X_{t}^{(i)}\big|^{1+\alpha}\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}+1}^{(1:n)}\right]\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\left[\big|X_{t}^{(i)}\big|^{1+\alpha}\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t^{\prime}-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(1:n)}\right]\bigg]\bigg\}
