Consider the evaluation function
g:\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})\times\mathcal{F}\mapsto\real
where
g(\mathbb{G},f)\triangleq\mathbb{G}(f)-\mathbb{G}(f_{0})
. Note that the evaluation mapping
g
is continuous at
\big(z,f\big)\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})\times\mathcal{F}
if
z
is continuous at
f
; this is discussed in the proof of Lemma 18.15 of
Van der Vaart 2000
. Since, as discussed earlier,
\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}
can be constructed to have almost all sample paths in
\textnormal{UC}(\mathcal{F},\rho)
, thus
\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}
is at
f_{0}
for almost all sample paths and the evaluation mapping
g
is continuous at
\big(\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}},f_{0}\big)
for almost all sample paths.

Thus, by the continuous mapping theorem, we have that

\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}^{(n)}\big(\hat{f}^{(n)}\big)-\mathbb{G}_{0}\big(f_{0}\big)=g\big(\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}^{(n)},\hat{f}^{(n)}\big)\overset{D}{\to}g\big(\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}},f_{0}\big)=\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(f_{0}\big)-\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(f_{0}\big)=0.

(D.38)

Since convergence in distribution to a constant implies convergence in probability, display (
D.38
) above implies the main result display (
D.35
) holds.

We now show that
\rho\big(\hat{f}^{(n)},f_{0}\big)\overset{P}{\to}0
.

Note that for any functions
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
,

\rho(f,g)^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big|\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(f)-\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(g)\big|^{2}\right]

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(f)^{2}-2\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(f)\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(g)+\mathbb{G}_{\mathcal{F}}(g)^{2}\right]

Using the covariance expression from display (
D.37
), we have that for

G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};f)\triangleq\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})]
,

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};f)^{2}-2G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};f)G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};g)+G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};g)^{2}\right]

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\left\{G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};f)-G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};g)\right\}^{2}\right]

By the definition of
G(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)};f)
,

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg\{\left(\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})]\right)\\
-\left(\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})]\right)\bigg\}^{2}\bigg]

Let
X=\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})
. Since
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[(X-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}[X])^{2}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[X^{2}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[X\big]^{2}
,

=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\left(\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\bigg]\\
-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]^{2}

\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-2}\left\{f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right\}^{2}\bigg]

By Condition
2
,
\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-2}\leq\pi_{\min}^{2(t-1)}
a.s., so

\leq\pi_{\min}^{2(t-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\left\{f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-g(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right\}^{2}\right]

The above implies that
\rho\big(\hat{f}^{(n)},f_{0}\big)\overset{P}{\to}0
because by assumption of the Lemma, we have that
\nu\big(\hat{f}^{(n)},f_{0}\big)\overset{P}{\to}0
where
\nu(f,g)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big|f\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}\big)-g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}\big)\big|^{2}\right]^{1/2}
.
~~~\blacksquare

Appendix E
Maximal Inequalities for Adaptively Sampled Data

Overview and Notation for Appendix
E
Results.

In this Appendix, we will use the following notation:

\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\triangleq\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}^{*}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\textnormal{~~and~~}\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\triangleq\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\hat{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}^{(n)}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)}).

Consider a function class
\mathcal{F}
whose complexity is sufficiently controlled. In this section, our goal is to show a maximal inequality to bound the following:

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right)\bigg|\bigg]

(E.1)

Above
\mathbb{E}^{*}
refers to outer expectations as defined in Section 18.2
Van der Vaart 2000
. A bound for the above term in display (
E.1
) is used in Theorem
D.5
(Functional Asymptotic Normality under Finite Bracketing Integral for Adaptively Sampled Data). This maximal inequality will be a function of the bracketing integral
\mathcal{F}
bracketing integral,
\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{p}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon
, which we assume is finite.

Note that since
\{\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are not independent in our setting, we cannot use classical maximal inequalities for i.i.d. data to bound the term in display (
E.1
). See Section 19.6 of
Van der Vaart 2000
for information on maximal inequalities for i.i.d. data. Our results build on the ideas used in these results for i.i.d. data.

Summary of Results in this Section

•

Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality (Lemma
E.2
)
proves a Bernstein inequality for our non-independent, adaptively sampled data type and is the most novel step in this section. The proof leverages the conditional independence of the action selection at each time-step and the fact that the underlying potential outcomes are i.i.d. The proof repeatedly uses a key helper Lemma
E.1
(Moving Products out of Expectations using Weights).

•

Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions (Lemma
E.3
)
proves a maximal inequality to bound the term in display (
E.1
) in the case that
|\mathcal{F}|<\infty
. The proof closely follows that of Lemma 19.33
Van der Vaart 2000
, but replaces the use of a Bernstein inequality for i.i.d. data with Lemma
E.2
(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality).

•

Maximal Inequality as a Function of the Bracketing Integral (Lemma
E.4
)
proves a maximal inequality to bound the term in display (
E.1
) as a function of the bracketing integral for
\mathcal{F}
. The proof closely follows that of Lemma 19.34
Van der Vaart 2000
, but replaces the use of a maximal inequality for empirical processes for a finite class of functions on i.i.d. data with Lemma
E.3
(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions).

E.1
Moving Products out of Expectations using Weights (Lemma
E.1
)

Lemma E.1

(Moving Products out of Expectations using Weights)
.

For any
t\in[2\colon T]
, let
f
be any real-valued, measurable function of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\big[\big|f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\big|\big]<\infty
. Let
c
be fixed constants. The following equality holds for any
n\geq 1
:

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})+c\right)\bigg]=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})+c\right].

(E.2)

Remark 10

(Display (
E.2
) Comment)
.

Note that regarding the expectation terms on the right hand side above, for any stochastic policies
\pi_{2:t}(\beta_{1:t-1})
,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}^{*}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\bigg)^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\bigg]\\
=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}(\beta_{1:t-1})}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\pi_{t^{\prime}}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)};\beta_{t^{\prime}-1})\bigg)^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\bigg].

Proof of Lemma
E.1
(Conditional Independence using Weights).

For notational convenience we consider the
t
set to
T
case; the argument holds by the same argument for any
t\in[2\colon T]
.

Let
t\in[2:T]
and let
g
be a real-valued, measurable function of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}
. A key result which we will take as given for now (we prove it at the end of this proof), is that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)+c\bigg)\bigg]\\
=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t-1}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\bigg[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)}\bigg]+c\bigg)\bigg].\hskip-2.84526pt

(E.3)

We now show that the desired result holds by repeatedly applying display (
E.3
).
Applying display (
E.3
) for
t
set to
T
and for
g
set to
f
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+c\right)\bigg]

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-1}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{T}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg]

Now, note that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{T}^{(i)}\right]
is a function of
\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{T}^{(i)}
; let this be function be
g
when we apply display (
E.3
) again for
t
set to
T-1
.

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-2}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T-1}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T-1}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{T}^{(i)}\right]\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{T-2}^{(i)},S_{T-1}^{(i)}\right]+c\bigg)\bigg]

Since
\big\{\pi_{T-1}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}
is a constant given
\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)}
,

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-2}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T-1}^{*}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T-1:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{T}^{(i)}\right]\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{T-2}^{(i)},S_{T-1}^{(i)}\right]+c\bigg)\bigg]

By law of iterated expectations,

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-2}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T-1:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T-1:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-2}^{(i)},S_{T-1}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg].

By repeatedly applying display (
E.3
) and the above argument for
t
set to
T-2,T-3,...,2
we have that

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-3}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T-2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T-2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{3}^{(i)},S_{T-2}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg]

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T-4}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{T-3:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{T-3:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{4}^{(i)},S_{T-3}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg]

=...=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)},S_{1}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg].

Finally, recall that
\big\{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)},S_{2}^{(i)}\big\}_{i=1}^{n}=\big\{S_{1}^{(i)},A_{1}^{(i)},Y_{1}^{(i)},S_{2}^{(i)}\big\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent over
i\in[1\colon n]
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)},S_{1}^{(i)}\right]+c\right\}\bigg]\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathcal{H}_{1}^{(i)},S_{1}^{(i)}\right]+c\right]

By law of iterated expectations,

=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+c\right].

Thus we have shown that the desired result holds and all that is left is to show that display (
E.3
) holds.

Proof of display (
E.3
).

The proof of display (
E.3
) leverages (i) the Radon-Nikodym weights and (ii) conditional independence properties. Pick any
t\in[2:T]
and let
g
be a real-valued, measurable function of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}
. By law of iterated expectations,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)+c\right)\bigg]

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)+c\right)\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\bigg]\bigg]

Note that the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)+c\right)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]
is only integrating over
\big\{A_{t}^{(i)},Y_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big\}_{i=1}^{n}
(the policy parameters
\hat{\beta}_{1:t-1}^{(n)}
used in
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}
are known given
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)}
). Additionally, note that conditional on
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}
,

\big\{A_{t}^{(i)},Y_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big\}
are independent over
i\in[1\colon n]
. Thus,

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]+c\right)\bigg]

Since
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t-1}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}=\big(\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t-1}\hat{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}^{(n)}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\big)^{-1}
is a constant given
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}
,

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t-1}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\bigg|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]+c\right)\bigg]

Now now show that the above equals the following:

=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t-1}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{T-1}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)}\right]+c\right)\bigg].

(E.4)

Showing the above will be sufficient for display (
E.3
).

Note that since
1=\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})^{-1}\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})=\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})g\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right].

The last equality above holds because
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\pi_{t}^{*}(A_{t}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)})=W_{t}^{(i)}(\beta_{t-1}^{*},\hat{\beta}_{t-1}^{(n)})
.

Also, note that the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]
integrates over
\big\{A_{t}^{(i)},Y_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big\}
. Since actions are selected using
\pi_{t}^{*}
rather than
\hat{\pi}_{t}^{(n)}
in the expectation, the distribution of
\big\{A_{t}^{(i)},Y_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big\}
depends only on
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}
through
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)}
. This means that

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(1:n)},S_{t}^{(1:n)}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g\big(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)},S_{t+1}^{(i)}\big)\big|\mathcal{H}_{t-1}^{(i)},S_{t}^{(i)}\right].

Thus we have shown display (
E.4
) holds.
\blacksquare

E.2
Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality (Lemma
E.2
)

Lemma E.2

(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality)
.

We assume Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration) holds.
Let
f
be a real-valued, measurable function of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
. Then, for any
x>0
and for all
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\bigg|\geq x\bigg)\\
\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{\pi_{\min}^{t-1}}{4}\frac{x^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})^{2}\right]+x\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

(E.5)

Above
\|f\|_{\infty}\triangleq\sup_{h}|f(h)|
.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality).

We follow an argument similar to Lemma 19.32 in
Van der Vaart 2000
. For notational convenience we consider the
t
set to
T
case; the argument holds by the same argument for any
t\in[2\colon T]
.

The leading 2 in display (
E.5
) is due to separate bounds for the upper and lower tail bounds. It is sufficient to show the upper tail bound, because the lower tail bound holds by the upper tail bound applied to
-f
.

Note if
\|f\|_{\infty}=0
, then
\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right)\geq x\right)=0
, so in this case display (
E.5
) easily holds. Thus, for the remainder of the proof we assume that
\|f\|_{\infty}>0
.

Let
x>0
.

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{\ \sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)\geq x\bigg)

(E.6)

Using a Chernoff bound, for any
\lambda>0
,

\leq e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg\{\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)\bigg\}\bigg]

Using properties of exponents, we can change the summation in exponent into a product,

=e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)\right\}\bigg]

We now apply Maclaurin series for exponential function, i.e., that
e^{z}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{k}}{k!}
.

=e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)^{k}\bigg]

By simplifying the first two terms in the inner summation,

=e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{1+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)^{k}\bigg\}\bigg]

(E.7)

Now note the following observations:

•

Let
\epsilon>0
. By Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration),

\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}=\bigg[\prod_{t^{\prime}=2}^{t}\hat{\pi}_{t^{\prime}}^{(n)}(A_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)},S_{t^{\prime}}^{(i)})\bigg]^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}~~\textnormal{a.s.},

(E.8)

and

\left|\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right|\\
\leq\left|\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right|\leq 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}.

(E.9)

•

We can upper bound the following:

\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}

=\big(\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big)^{-2}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}-2\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}

Note that
\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]
.

=\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}-2f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)\\
+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}

Since
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}
a.s. by display (
E.8
),

\leq\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\bigg(\underbrace{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}-2f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]}_{\triangleq g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})}\bigg)\\
+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}

For
g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\triangleq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}-2f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]
,

=\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}.

Thus, in summary we have that

\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}\\
\leq\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}.

(E.10)

•

By display (
E.9
) and (
E.10
), we have that for any
k\geq 2

\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{k}\\
\leq\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}\left|\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right|^{k-2}\\
\leq\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)^{2}\bigg)\big\{2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\big\}^{k-2}

(E.11)

Note that in display (
E.7
), each of the terms in the product over
n
terms is non-negative because the
i^{\textnormal{th}}
term in the product equals
\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right)\right\}
and
e^{x}\geq 0
for all
x
. Thus, by display (
E.11
), we can upper bound display (
E.7
) as follows:

\leq e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{1+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}\bigg)\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg\}\bigg]

(E.12)

Note that everything in the expectation above in display (
E.12
) is bounded a.s.; we will show that this is true for the infinite summation over
k
. Let
y=\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}
and
z=2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}
. Note that both
y
and
z
are bounded a.s. Thus, since
\|f\|_{\infty}>0
by assumption (we discussed the
\|f\|_{\infty}=0
case at the beginning of this proof), we have that
z>0
, so
\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\big)^{k}yz^{k-2}=yz^{-2}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\big)^{k}z^{k}=yz^{-2}\left(e^{z\lambda/\sqrt{n}}-\sum_{k=0}^{1}\frac{1}{k!}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\big)^{k}z^{k}\right)
is also bounded a.s.

Moreover, display (
E.12
) can be written as
e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}h(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+c\right)\right]
, for some function
h
and some finite constant
c
. (Note that
\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]
)
Thus, we can apply Lemma
E.1
(Moving Products out of Expectations using Weights) to get that display (
E.12
) is equal to

=e^{-\lambda x}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[1+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\bigg)\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}\bigg)\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg].

Since
\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]
, we can cancel terms in the first line above.

=e^{-\lambda x}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{1+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\bigg[\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}\bigg)\\
\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg]\bigg\}

Since everything in the expectations above are bounded a.s. (discussed below display (
E.12
)), we can exchange the expectation with the infinite summation over
k
.

=e^{-\lambda x}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}\bigg)\\
\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg\}.

(E.13)

Note the following:

•

Recall that
g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\triangleq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}-2f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]
. So,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}

=\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}

=\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]^{2}

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]

Thus display (
E.13
) can be upper bounded by the following:

e^{-\lambda x}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg\}

By i.i.d. potential outcomes,

=e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{k}\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]\left(2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}\right)^{k-2}\bigg\}^{n}

By rearranging terms,

=e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{k}\underbrace{2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}_{\triangleq\lambda_{1}^{-1}}\bigg(\underbrace{2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}}_{\triangleq\lambda_{2}^{-1}}\bigg)^{k-2}\bigg\}^{n}

=e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{k}\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}\lambda_{2}^{-(k-2)}\right)\bigg\}^{n}

(E.14)

Note that since
\lambda_{1}^{-1},\lambda_{2}^{-1}\geq 0
,

\lambda\triangleq x\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}+x\lambda_{2}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\leq\min\left\{x\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}+0\right)^{-1},x\left(0+x\lambda_{2}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\}=\min\left(x\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\right).

Thus we have that
\lambda^{k}\leq\lambda\min(x\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})^{k-1}\leq\lambda x\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}^{k-2}
. So we can upper bound display (
E.14
) as follows:

\leq e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\frac{1}{2}\left(\lambda x\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}^{k-2}\right)\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}\lambda_{2}^{-(k-2)}\right)\bigg\}^{n}

=e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\frac{1}{n}\underbrace{\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}}_{\leq 1}\frac{1}{2}\lambda x\bigg\}^{n}

By the Maclaurin series for exponential function,
e^{z}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{k}}{k!}
, we have
\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}=e-\frac{1}{0!}-\frac{1}{1!}=e-2\leq 1
.

\leq e^{-\lambda x}\bigg\{1+\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{2}x\lambda\bigg\}^{n}

Again by the Maclaurin series for exponential function,
e^{z}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{k}}{k!}
, so for
z>0
we have that
1+z\leq e^{z}
. This means that
(1+z)^{n}\leq e^{zn}
.

\leq e^{-\lambda x}\exp\bigg(\frac{1}{2}x\lambda\bigg)=\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}x\lambda\bigg)

Recall that
\lambda\triangleq x\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}+x\lambda_{2}^{-1}\right)^{-1}
, so,

=\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}xx\left(\lambda_{1}^{-1}+x\lambda_{2}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\bigg)

Recall that
\lambda_{1}^{-1}=2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]
and
\lambda_{2}^{-1}=2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}
. So,

=\exp\bigg(-\frac{\pi_{\min}^{T-1}}{4}\frac{x^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\big[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\big]+x\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}}\bigg).~~\blacksquare

E.3
Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions (Lemma
E.3
)

Lemma E.3

(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a finite class of bounded, real-valued, measurable functions of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
with size
|\mathcal{F}|\geq 2
. For
f\in\mathcal{F}
we define

\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\triangleq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right).

Under Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration) for all sufficiently large
n
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\big|\bigg]\leq C\bigg\{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\log(|\mathcal{F}|)\\
+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(t-1)}}\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\log(|\mathcal{F}|)}\bigg\},

(E.15)

for some universal positive constant
C
(specified in the proof).

Proof of Lemma
E.3
(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions).
Our proof follows a very similar argument to Lemma 19.33 in
Van der Vaart 2000
. Specifically, our proof only deviates because we use our Lemma
E.2
(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality) to prove displays (
E.19
) and (
E.21
) below.

For notational convenience we consider the
t
set to
T
case; the argument holds by the same argument for any
t\in[2\colon T]
.

Special cases.

•

Note that if
f\in\mathcal{F}
such that
\|f\|_{\infty}=0
, then
\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)=0
. These zero functions do not contribute to increasing the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\big|\big]
. Thus, we assume that
\|f\|_{\infty}>0
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
for the remainder of this proof, as this is the most difficult case.

•

Note that
f\in\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]=0
, then
f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})
is a constant function and
\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)=0
. These constant functions do not contribute to increasing the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\big|\big]
. Thus, we assume that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]>0
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
for the remainder of this proof, as this is the most difficult case.

Main argument.

Let
u,v
be non-negative, real-valued functions of
f\in\mathcal{F}
such that

•

u(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]

•

v(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}

Note that

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\bigg\{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|>u(f)/v(f)}+|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\leq u(f)/v(f)}\bigg\}\right]

\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|>u(f)/v(f)}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\leq u(f)/v(f)}\right]

Let
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\triangleq|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|>u(f)/v(f)}
and
\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\triangleq|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\leq u(f)/v(f)}
.

=\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right]

\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/v(f)\right]\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}v(f)\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/\sqrt{u(f)}\right]\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{u(f)}\right)

(E.16)

The main results show in this proof are the following:

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/v(f)\right]\leq\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)

(E.17)

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/\sqrt{u(f)}\right]\leq\sqrt{\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)}

(E.18)

For now we take displays (
E.17
) and (
E.18
) as given and show why the Lemma holds. Using these two results, we have that display (
E.16
) can be upper bounded by the following:

\leq\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)\bigg(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\underbrace{24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}}_{v(f)}\bigg)\\
+\sqrt{\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)}\bigg(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\underbrace{\sqrt{24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}}_{\sqrt{u(f)}}\bigg)

=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)\\
+\sqrt{24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\right)\sqrt{\log\left(1+|\mathcal{F}|\right)}

Since
c_{\log}\triangleq\sup_{x\geq 2}\frac{\log(1+x)}{\log(x)}
is bounded,
\frac{\log(1+|\mathcal{F}|)}{\log(|\mathcal{F}|)}\leq c_{\log}
so,
1\leq c_{\log}\frac{\log(|\mathcal{F}|)}{\log(1+|\mathcal{F}|)}
.

\leq c_{\log}24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|\right)\\
+\sqrt{c_{\log}24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|\right)}\right)

\leq 24\max\big(c_{\log},c_{\log}^{1/2}\big)\bigg\{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|\right)\\
+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\right)\sqrt{\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|\right)}\bigg\}.

The above implies that the desired result, display (
E.15
), holds. All that remains is to prove that displays (
E.17
) and (
E.18
) hold.

Proving display (
E.17
) holds.

Let
x>0
. We now state some results and discuss why they hold below. For all
n\geq 1
we have that for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}}_{n}(f)\right|\geq x\right)\underbrace{\leq}_{(a)}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}}_{n}(f)\right|\geq\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}\right)\\
\underbrace{\leq}_{(b)}2\exp\bigg(-6\frac{\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}^{2}}{u(f)+\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}v(f)}\bigg)\underbrace{\leq}_{(c)}2\exp\left(-3\frac{x}{v(f)}\right).

(E.19)

•

Inequality (a) holds because recall that
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\triangleq|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|>u(f)/v(f)}
.

•

Inequality (b) holds by Lemma
E.2
(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality) since Condition
2
holds. Recall that
u(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]
and
v(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}
.

•

Inequality (c) holds because

6\frac{\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}^{2}}{u(f)+\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}v(f)}=6\frac{\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}}{u(f)/\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}+v(f)}\\
\geq 6\frac{x}{u(f)/\max\big\{x,u(f)/v(f)\big\}+v(f)}\geq 6\frac{x}{u(f)/\big\{u(f)/v(f)\big\}+v(f)}=3\frac{x}{v(f)}.

We now show that the following is less than or equal to
1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)}\right]-1=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)}e^{x}dx\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{x\leq\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)}e^{x}dx\right]

Note the following:

•

Since
f
is bounded and since
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{T-1}
a.s. by Condition
2
, thus
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)
is bounded a.s. (remember
n
is fixed).

•

Since we are consider the cases in which
\|f\|_{\infty}>0
, thus
v(f)>0
.

•

By the above two results,
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/v(f)
is bounded a.s., so
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{x\leq\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)}e^{x}dx\right]
is also bounded.

Thus, by Fubini’s theorem, we can exchange the integrals,

=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{I}_{x\leq\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)}\bigg]e^{x}dx=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|\geq xv(f)\bigg)e^{x}dx

By display (
E.19
),

\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-3x}e^{x}dx=2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2x}dx=2\left(\lim_{x\to\infty}-\frac{1}{2}e^{-2x}+\frac{1}{2}e^{0}\right)=2\left(0+\frac{1}{2}\right)=1.

Thus we have that for
\gamma(x)=e^{x}-1
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma\big\{\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\big\}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\big\{\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\big\}\right]-1\leq 1.

(E.20)

Note that
\gamma(x)=e^{x}-1
is convex,
so by Jensen’s inequality,

\exp\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\right]\right)-1=\gamma\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\right]\right)

\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\right)\right]\leq\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma\left\{\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\right\}\right]\leq|\mathcal{F}|.

The last inequality above holds by display (
E.20
).
By adding
1
and taking the
\log
on both sides, we have display (
E.17
) holds, i.e., that

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/v(f)\right]\leq\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|+1\right).

Proving display (
E.18
) holds.

Let
x>0
. We now state some results and discuss why they hold below. For all
n\geq 1
we have that for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{n}(f)\right|\geq x\right)\underbrace{=}_{(a)}\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{n}(f)\right|\geq x\right)&\textnormal{~if~}x<u(f)/v(f)\\
0&\textnormal{~if~}x\geq u(f)/v(f)\end{cases}

(E.21)

\underbrace{\leq}_{(b)}\begin{cases}2\exp\left(-6\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)+xv(f)}\right)&\textnormal{~if~}x<u(f)/v(f)\\
0&\textnormal{~if~}x\geq u(f)/v(f)\end{cases}

\underbrace{\leq}_{(c)}\begin{cases}2\exp\left(-3\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)}\right)&\textnormal{~if~}x<u(f)/v(f)\\
0&\textnormal{~if~}x\geq u(f)/v(f)\end{cases}\leq 2\exp\left(-3\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)}\right).

•

Inequality (a) holds because recall that
\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\triangleq|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\mathbb{I}_{|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)|\leq u(f)/v(f)}
.

•

Inequality (b) holds by Lemma
E.2
(Weighted Martingale Bernstein Inequality) since Condition
2
holds. Recall that
u(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]
and
v(f)=24\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\|f\|_{\infty}/\sqrt{n}
.

•

Inequality (c) holds because if
x<u(f)/v(f)
, then

6\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)+xv(f)}\geq 6\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)+u(f)/v(f)v(f)}=3\frac{x^{2}}{u(f)}.

We now show that the following is less than or equal to
1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|^{2}/u(f)}\right]-1=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|^{2}/u(f)}e^{x}dx\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{x\leq\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|^{2}/u(f)}e^{x}dx\right]

=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{\sqrt{xu(f)}\leq\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|}e^{x}dx\right]

Note the following:

•

Since
f
is bounded and since
\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\leq\pi_{\min}^{T-1}
a.s. by Condition
2
, thus
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)
is bounded a.s. (remember
n
is fixed).

•

Since are considering the cases in which
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]>0
(see discussion of special cases at the beginning of this proof), thus
u(f)>0
.

•

By the above two results,
\underline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)/\sqrt{u(f)}
is bounded a.s., so
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{\sqrt{xu(f)}\leq\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|}e^{x}dx\right]
is also bounded.

Thus, by Fubini’s theorem, we can exchange integrals,

=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{I}_{\sqrt{xu(f)}\leq\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|}\right]e^{x}dx=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|\geq\sqrt{xu(f)}\right)e^{x}dx

By display (
E.21
),

\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-3x+x}dx=2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2x}dx=2\left(\lim_{x\to\infty}-\frac{1}{2}e^{-2x}+\frac{1}{2}e^{0}\right)=2\left(0+\frac{1}{2}\right)=1.

Thus we have that for
\gamma_{2}(x)=e^{x^{2}}-1
,

\gamma_{2}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|^{2}/u(f)\right)\right]-1\leq 1.

(E.22)

Since
\gamma_{2}(x)=e^{x^{2}}-1
is convex, by Jensen’s inequality,

\exp\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\right]^{2}\bigg)-1=\gamma_{2}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\bigg]\bigg)

\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\gamma_{2}\left(\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\right)\bigg]\leq\sum_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\gamma_{2}\left(\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\right)\bigg]\leq|\mathcal{F}|.

The last inequality above holds by display (
E.22
). By adding
1
, taking the
\log
and the square-root on both sides, we have display (
E.18
) holds, i.e., that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\overline{\mathbb{G}_{n}}(f)\right|/\sqrt{u(f)}\bigg]\leq\sqrt{\log\left(|\mathcal{F}|+1\right)}.~~~\blacksquare

E.4
Maximal Inequality as a Function of the Bracketing Integral (Lemma
E.4
)

Lemma E.4

(Maximal Inequality as a Function of the Bracketing Integral)
.

Let
\delta>0
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of real-valued measurable functions of
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)}
such that

\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\big[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\big]\leq\delta^{2}
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
.

Under Condition
2
(Minimum Exploration), for all
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\big|\bigg]\lesssim\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}}\big)\big)}d\epsilon\\
+\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:t}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:t}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}F(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right],

(E.23)

where

•

a(\delta)=\delta/\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}

•

\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\triangleq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})\right]\right)

•

F
is an envelope where
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})|<F(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})<\infty
with probability
1
.

Above
\lesssim
means less than or equal to when scaled by universal positive constants. Above
\mathbb{E}^{*}
refers to outer expectations as defined in Section 18.2
Van der Vaart 2000
.

Proof of Lemma
E.4
(Maximal Inequality as a Function of the Bracketing Integral).

Our proof is almost identical to that of Lemma 19.34
Van der Vaart 2000
except that we use the maximal inequality in Lemma
E.3
instead of a maximal inequality for i.i.d. data; we include the full proof for clarity and completeness. For notational convenience we consider the
t
set to
T
case; the argument holds by the same argument for any
t\in[2\colon T]
.

Note that by triangle inequality,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\right|\bigg]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(f\mathbb{I}_{F>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right)\right|\bigg]+\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(f\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right)\right|\bigg].

(E.24)

Bounding First Term in display (
E.24
).

This term is to deal with potentially unbounded functions
f\in\mathcal{F}
.

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(f\mathbb{I}_{F>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right)\right|\bigg]

By using the definition of
\mathbb{G}_{n}
,

=\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]\bigg)\bigg|\bigg]

By triangle inequality,

\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right|\bigg]\\
+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\{\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]\right|\right\}

By Jensen’s inequality,

\leq 2\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\big|f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right)\bigg]

Recall our envelope function
F
satisfies
|f(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})|<F(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(i)})<\infty
a.s., so

\leq 2\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:T}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]

=2\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]

Since the expectation above is indexed by the deterministic policy
\pi_{2:T}^{*}
,
\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)}
within the expectation are i.i.d.

=2\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]

This us gives us the second part of the bound from display (
E.23
).

Bounding Second Term in display (
E.24
).

We now focus on bounding the following:

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}f\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right|\bigg]

We now consider the class of functions
\bar{\mathcal{F}}:=\left\{f\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}:f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}
.
We first show that

N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\leq N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)
.

•

By definition of bracketing numbers, we can cover
\mathcal{F}
with
N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)
brackets, each with size at most
\epsilon
. Specifically, we can find brackets
[l_{j},u_{j}]
for

j\in\left[1\colon N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\right]
that cover
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[(u_{j}-l_{j})^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\epsilon
for all brackets
[l_{j},u_{j}]
.

•

Note that brackets
\left[l_{j}\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)},~u_{j}\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right]
for some
j\in\left[1\colon N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\right]
cover
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
.

•

Additionally, note that
\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big([u_{j}-l_{j}]\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\big)^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[(u_{j}-l_{j})^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.

Thus, we have that

N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\leq N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big).

(E.25)

Desiderata for Nested Partitions.

We now assume the existence of nested partitions of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
that satisfy certain conditions. We will finish the proof assuming these partitions exist and conclude by constructing these partitions.

High level, we assume we have nested partitions of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
that are indexed by positive integers
q
. These partitions are designed to become increasingly fine-grained as
q
increases. Specifically the “size” of each piece of the partition will be on the order of
2^{-q}
, i.e., the “size” of the partitions will halve as
q
increases by
1
. The partitions are nested in that each partition piece at level
q+1
is a subset of some partition piece at level
q
.

We pick
q_{0}
to be a positive integer such that

\delta<2^{-q_{0}}\leq 2\delta.

(E.26)

For every integer
q\geq q_{0}
we have a partition of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
; we denote these partions as
\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
. We assume that
N_{q_{0}}=N_{[~]}\big(2^{-q_{0}},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)
. These partitions are nested in that for each
q\geq q_{0}+1
and for every
j\in[1\colon N_{q}]
, we have that the partition piece
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
is a subset of some partition piece
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q-1,k}
for some
k\in[1\colon N_{q-1}]
. Moreover, we further assume the following:

•

Requirement on the “size” of partition pieces:

For each partition
q
and partition piece
j\in[1\colon N_{q}]
, let
\Delta_{q,j}
be a measurable function of
\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)}
such that
\sup_{f,g\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}}|f-g|\leq\Delta_{q,j}
a.s. and

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]\leq 2^{-2q}.

(E.27)

•

Requirement on how the number of partition pieces grows as the “size” goes to zero:

\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon.

(E.28)

We construct nested partitions that satisfy the above conditions at the end of this proof. For now, we assume such nested partitions described above exist and we continue with the argument.

Main Argument Assuming Desired Nested Partitions Exist.

For every partition piece
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
, we choose a arbitrary point
\bar{f}_{q,j}
in that partition piece, i.e., for each
q\geq q_{0}
and every
j\in[1\colon N_{q}]
we choose a point
\bar{f}_{q,j}\in\mathcal{F}_{q,j}
. We also define functions
\lambda_{q}:\bar{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto\bar{\mathcal{F}}
that maps each function
\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}
to these points
\{\bar{f}_{q,j}\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
; specifically, for any
\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}
we can find some partition piece
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
such that
\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
and we map that
\bar{f}
to the point
\bar{f}_{q,j}
.

Note that for any integer
Q>q_{0}
, by telescoping series, for any
\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}
,

\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})=\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{Q}\left\{\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right\}+\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\lambda_{Q+1}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})

=\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q}\left\{\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right\}\\
+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{q=Q+1}\left\{\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right\}.

(E.29)

For any
\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}
, we define
Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\in[q_{0},\infty]
to be a random variable representing the maximum partition level with no bound violations up to that level. Specifically,

Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\triangleq\bigg\{\sup_{q\geq q_{0}}\textnormal{ s.t. }\sum_{j=1}^{N_{p}}\mathbb{I}_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{p,j}}\Delta_{p,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\leq\sqrt{n}2^{-p}/\sqrt{\log N_{p}}\textnormal{ for all }p\in[q_{0}:q]\bigg\},

(E.30)

Thus, by replacing
Q
with
Q_{\bar{f}}
and by applying
\mathbb{G}_{n}
to both sides of display (
E.29
),

\mathbb{G}_{n}(\bar{f})=\mathbb{G}_{n}(\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f})+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}+1}\left(\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)

Thus, we have that by triangle inequality

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}f\mathbb{I}_{F\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\right|\bigg]=\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\bar{f})\right|\bigg]\\
\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f})\right|\bigg]}_{(i)}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]}_{(ii)}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}+1}\left(\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg].}_{(iii)}

(E.31)

Below we will show the following results:

•

Bounding term (i)

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f})\right|\bigg]\lesssim 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-q_{0}}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}

(E.32)

•

Bounding term (ii)

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\big(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\big)\bigg|\bigg]\lesssim 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}

(E.33)

•

Bounding term (iii)

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}+1}\left(\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]\lesssim 4\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

(E.34)

For now we assume the above three displays hold (we show they hold later). Thus, we can upper bound display (
E.31
) as follows:

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\bar{f})\right|\bigg]\lesssim 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-q_{0}}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}+6\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}

\leq 8\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}

By display (
E.28
),

\lesssim\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log(N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log(N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon.

The last inequality above holds by display (
E.25
).
We now show that displays (
E.32
), (
E.33
), and (
E.34
) hold.

Display (
E.32
): Bounding term (i) .

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\lambda_{q_{0}}\bar{f})\right|\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q_{0}}]}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(\bar{f}_{q_{0},j})\right|\bigg]

By Lemma
E.3
(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions) for any
n\geq 1
,

\lesssim\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q_{0}}]}\frac{\|\bar{f}_{q_{0},j}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\log N_{q_{0}}\\
+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q_{0}}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\bar{f}_{q_{0},j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}.

(E.35)

•

Note that since
\bar{f}_{q_{0},j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})=f_{q_{0},j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\mathbb{I}_{F(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)}\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)
a.s., we get the first inequality below:

\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q_{0}}]}\big\{\|\bar{f}_{q_{0},j}\|_{\infty}\big\}\leq\sqrt{n}a(\delta)=\sqrt{n}\delta/\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}\leq\sqrt{n}2^{-q_{0}}/\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}.

The last inequality above holds because
\delta<2^{-q_{0}}\leq 2\delta
from display (
E.26
) and

N_{[~]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\geq N_{[~]}\big(2^{-q_{0}},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)\geq N_{[~]}\big(2^{-q_{0}},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)=N_{q_{0}}.

The last inequality above holds by display (
E.25
).

•

\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\bar{f}_{q_{0},j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\leq\delta\leq 2^{-q_{0}}
; the first inequality holds by assumption of the Lemma and the second inequality holds since we choose
q_{0}
such that
\delta<2^{-q_{0}}\leq 2\delta
.

Thus, by the above bullets, we can upper bound display (
E.35
) as follows:

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-q_{0}}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}2^{-q_{0}}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}\leq 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-q_{0}}\sqrt{\log N_{q_{0}}}.

Thus, we have that display (
E.32
) holds.

Bounding term (ii):.

By triangle inequality,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\big(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\big)\bigg|\bigg]\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]

By the definition of
Q_{\bar{f}}
from display (
E.30
),
\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}}=\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\lambda_{q}\bar{f}}}
,

=\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}-\bar{f}_{q,j}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]

By Lemma
E.3
(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions) for all sufficiently large
n
,

\lesssim\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q_{0}}]}\frac{\left\|\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}-\bar{f}_{q,j}\right)\right\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{n}}\log N_{q}\\
+\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}-\bar{f}_{q,j}\right)^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

(E.36)

•

Note that by the definition of
\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}
(
Q_{\bar{f}}
was defined in display (
E.30
)) and by our nested partitions, we have that

\left\|\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}-\bar{f}_{q,j}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}}\left\|\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}|f-f^{\prime}|\right\|_{\infty}\\
\leq\left\|\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{n}2^{-q}/\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

•

By our nested partitions, we have that
\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f},\lambda_{q}\bar{f}
are in the same
q^{\mathrm{th}}
-level partition piece, i.e.,
\lambda_{q+1}\bar{f},\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
for some
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
with
j\in[1\colon N_{q}]
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})}\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\bar{f}_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\right]\\
\leq\sup_{\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\left(\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\bar{f}^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right].

Moreover, by properties of our partitions,

\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})}\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\left(\bar{f}_{q+1,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\bar{f}_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\right]}\\
\leq\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]}\leq 2^{-q}.

The last inequality above holds by the size property of our partitions from display (
E.27
).

By the above bullets, we have that display (
E.36
) is upper bounded by the following:

\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\left\{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\right\}\leq 2\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

Thus, we have that display (
E.33
) holds.

Bounding term (iii):.

By triangle inequality,

\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}+1}\left(\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}+1}\left(\bar{f}-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}\right)\right)\bigg|\bigg]

(E.37)

Note that if some functions
f,g
are such that
\big|f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big|\leq g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})
a.s., then

\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(f)\right|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right|+\sqrt{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}f(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big]\right|\\
\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right|+\sqrt{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right|\\
\leq\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}(g)\right|+2\sqrt{n}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}g(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right]\right|\textnormal{~~a.s.}

(E.38)

Note that
\left|\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\big(\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-\lambda_{q}\bar{f}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big)\right|\leq\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{q}}\mathbb{I}_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})
a.s. by the definition of
\Delta_{q,j}
from above display (
E.27
). So by the observation from display (
E.38
) we can upper bound display (
E.37
) as follows:

\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bigg(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{q}}\mathbb{I}_{\bar{f}\in\mathcal{F}_{q,j}}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\bigg)\bigg|\bigg]\\
+2\sqrt{n}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}}\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{q}}\mathbb{I}_{\bar{f}\in\mathcal{F}_{q,j}}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\bigg]\bigg|

Since
\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\bar{f}}+1}=\mathbb{I}_{q\leq Q_{\lambda_{q}\bar{f}}+1}
(
Q_{\bar{f}}
was defined in display (
E.30
)),

=\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}~\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)\bigg|\bigg]\\
+2\sqrt{n}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\bigg]

(E.39)

•

Due to the nested property of our partitions (see above display (
E.27
) for the definition of
\Delta_{q,j}
),

\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}~\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\leq\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}~\Delta_{q-1,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})~~\textnormal{a.s.}

Moreover, by the definition of
Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}
from display (
E.30
),

\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}~\Delta_{q-1,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\leq\sqrt{n}2^{-(q-1)}/\sqrt{\log N_{q-1}}~~\textnormal{a.s.}

Thus, by Lemma
E.3
(Maximal Inequality for Finite Class of Functions) and display (
E.27
),

\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{*}\bigg[\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\bigg|\mathbb{G}_{n}\big(\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}~\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big)\bigg|\bigg]\\
\lesssim\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\bigg\{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-(q-1)}/\sqrt{\log N_{q-1}}\log N_{q}+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\bigg\}

Since
N_{q-1}\leq N_{q}
(bracketing number for brackets of size
2^{-(q-1)}
vs
2^{-q}
), thus

\log N_{q-1}\leq\log N_{q}
and
1\leq\sqrt{\log N_{q}/\log N_{q-1}}
.
So,

\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\bigg\{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2^{-(q-1)}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}+\sqrt{\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\bigg\}\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

•

Note that by our nested partitions and by the definition of
Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}
from display (
E.30
), if
\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}=1
, then
\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})>\sqrt{n}2^{-q}/\sqrt{\log N_{q}}
. Thus, when
\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}=1
,
\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\big(\sqrt{n}2^{-q}/\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\big)^{-1}\geq 1
. Thus,

\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\leq\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\big(\sqrt{n}2^{-q}/\sqrt{\log N_{q}}\big)^{-1}.

Thus we have that

2\sqrt{n}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{q=Q_{\bar{f}_{q,j}}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})+1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\bigg]\bigg|\\
\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{2\sqrt{\log N_{q}}}{2^{-q}}\max_{j\in[1\colon N_{q}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\{\hat{\pi}_{2:t}^{(i)}\big\}^{-1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\bigg]\\
\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{2\sqrt{\log N_{q}}}{2^{-q}}\cdot 2^{-2q}=2\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}.

The last inequality above holds by the size property of our partitions from display (
E.27
).

By the observations in the above bullets, we can upper bound display (
E.39
) as follows:

\lesssim\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}2\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}+2\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}

\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}4\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}

Thus, we have that display (
E.34
) holds.

Construct nested partitions.

We now construct nested partitions that satisfy the conditions described previously, particularly displays (
E.27
) and (
E.28
).

By our bracketing number assumption, for every integer
q\geq q_{0}
, we can find

N_{q}^{*}\triangleq N_{[~]}\big(2^{-q}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)
bracketing functions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j}^{*},u_{q,j}^{*}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
of size at most
2^{-q}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2}
that cover
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
, i.e.,

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\left(u_{q,j}^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-l_{q,j}^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq 2^{-q}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2}.

(E.40)

These brackets form a partition of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
, which we write as
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}^{*}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
. Note that these partitions are not necessarily nested. Below we use
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}^{*}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
to refer to the potentially
non-nested
partitions (that exist by assumption) and
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
to refer to the
nested
partitions (that we will construct).

We take intersections of the partitions
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}^{*}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
to construct a set of nested partitions
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
for all integers
q\geq q_{0}
.

•

For partition
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0},j}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q_{0}}}
, we simply set
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0},j}\triangleq\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0},j}^{*}
for all
j\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}}^{*}\big]
. This means that
N_{q_{0}}\triangleq N_{q_{0}}^{*}
.

•

For partition
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,j}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q_{0}+1}}
, we set partition pieces
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,j}
for all
j\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}}^{*}\big]
to be the intersections between all pairs of partition pieces
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0},k}^{*}
and
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,l}^{*}
for
k\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}}\big]
and
l\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}+1}\big]
. This means that
N_{q_{0}+1}\triangleq N_{q_{0}}^{*}\cdot N_{q_{1}}^{*}
.
Note that it could be that some partition pieces
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,j}
are empty; this is okay, as we simply want to upper bound the bracketing number.

•

For general
q\geq q_{0}
, we set partition pieces
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
for
j\in\big[1\colon N_{q}\big]
to be the intersections between all possible combinations in which we take one partition piece from each partition level
\big\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{p,j}^{*}\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{p}^{*}}
for each
p\in[q_{0}\colon q]
. This means that each partition piece
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q,j}
is the intersection between
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0},k_{q_{0}}}^{*}
,
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,k_{q_{0}+1}}^{*}
, …,
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{q_{0}+1,k_{q}}^{*}
for
k_{q_{0}}\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}}\big]
,
k_{q_{0}+1}\in\big[1\colon N_{q_{0}}+1\big]
, …,
k_{q}\in\big[1\colon N_{q}\big]
.
This means that there are
N_{q}\triangleq\prod_{p=q_{0}}^{q}N_{p}^{*}
total partition pieces (for our constructed nested partitions) at this level.

Recall the potentially non-nested partitions were defined by bracketing functions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j}^{*},u_{q,j}^{*}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
. Due to how we constructed the nested partitions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j},u_{q,j}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
(procedure described above), we have the following results:

•

The nested partitions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j},u_{q,j}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
must be covering since the non-nested prtitions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j}^{*},u_{q,j}^{*}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
are covering and we took all possible intersections of these non-nested partitions of size
p\in[q_{0}:q]
to construct our nested partitions.

•

The nested partitions
\big\{\big[l_{q,j},u_{q,j}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}}
must be at most of the size of the largest bracket out of
\big\{\big[l_{q,j}^{*},u_{q,j}^{*}\big]\big\}_{j=1}^{N_{q}^{*}}
again since we took all possible intersections of these non-nested partitions of size
p\in[q_{0}:q]
to construct our nested partitions. Thus, we define
\Delta_{q,j}\triangleq u_{q,j}^{*}-l_{q,j}^{*}
; note that this choice of
\Delta_{q,j}
satisfies the conditions of display (
E.27
) since

\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\big\{\pi_{2:T}^{*,(i)}\big\}^{-1}\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]\leq\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\Delta_{q,j}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})^{2}\right]\\
=\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)}\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{2:T}^{*}}\left[\left(u_{q,j}^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})-l_{q,j}^{*}(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(i)})\right)^{2}\right]\leq 2^{-2q}.

The first inequality above holds by Condition
2
and the second inequality holds by how we defined the non-nested partitions in display (
E.40
).

We now show that display (
E.28
) holds, i.e., the number of sets in the partition grows at a bounded rate as the size of the partition pieces goes to zero:

\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log N_{q}}=\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\log\bigg(\prod_{p=q_{0}}^{q}N_{p}^{*}\bigg)}=\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sqrt{\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{q}\log N_{p}^{*}}

Note that
\sqrt{\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{q}\log N_{p}^{*}}\leq\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{q}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}
because
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for any positive non-negative values
a,b
.

\leq\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{q}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}=\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{I}_{p\leq q}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}=\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\mathbb{I}_{p\leq q}

For the last equality above, we can exchange the infinite summations above by Fubini’s theorem because the following argument will show that
\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\mathbb{I}_{p\leq q}
is bounded.

Since
\sum_{q=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\mathbb{I}_{p\leq q}=\sum_{q=p}^{\infty}2^{-q}=2^{-(p-1)}
,

=\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-(p-1)}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}=4\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-(p+1)}\sqrt{\log N_{p}^{*}}

=4\sum_{p=q_{0}}^{\infty}2^{-(p+1)}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(2^{-p}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}

Since
N_{[~]}\big(2^{-p}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)
is monotonically increasing as
p
increases by lower Darboux sums, we have the following upper bound:

\leq 4\int_{0}^{2^{-q_{0}}}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon

Since we chose
q_{0}
such that
\delta<2^{-q_{0}}\leq 2\delta
,

\leq 4\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\bar{\mathcal{F}},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon\underbrace{\leq}_{(a)}4\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}d\epsilon\\
=4\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)/2}\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(\epsilon\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2}d\epsilon\\
\underbrace{=}_{(b)}4\pi_{\min}^{-(T-1)/2}\int_{0}^{\delta\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2}}\sqrt{\log N_{[~]}\big(u,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{*}})\big)}du\underbrace{<}_{(c)}\infty.

Inequality (a) above holds by display (
E.25
).

Equality (b) above holds by integration by substitution for
u=\pi_{\min}^{(T-1)/2}\epsilon
.

Inequality (c) above holds by our finite bracketing integral assumption.
\blacksquare
